{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
depends. Specifically, we consider a change of variables from
(u,u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij})
to
(Q_{ij},u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij})
.
Since the quad polynomial
Q_{ij}
is multi-affine, we can write it as

Q_{ij}(u,u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})=r(u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})u+s(u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}),

so it is possible to write
u
as a rational function of
Q_{ij},u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij}
:

u=V(Q_{ij},u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}):=\frac{Q_{ij}-s(u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})}{r(u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})}\,.

In the case of the cross square 2-form, we can consider the Lagrangian on a single square, apply this variable transformation, and then Taylor expand about
Q_{ij}=0

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{n!}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{Q_{ij}}^{n}}\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\big(V(Q_{ij},u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}),u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}\big)\Big|_{Q_{ij}=0}\right)\,Q_{ij}^{n}\,.

Now, the fact that for
Q_{ij}=0
there holds
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

implies that the the first order term vanishes. Hence we have:

Cross 2-form.

Recall that
T,\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!=0
implies the existence of
\boldsymbol{\Theta}
and
\boldsymbol{\Xi}
such that
S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

(see Theorem
8
) and
\nabla S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}

{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Xi}}=0
, where
\nabla
denotes the gradient with respect to
u,\ldots,u_{ijk}
(these are the corner equations, see Proposition
3
).
With that in mind, and with the intention of finding a series expansion about
T,\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!=0
, we consider a change of variables form
(u,u_{i},u_{j},u_{k},u_{ij},u_{jk},u_{ki},u_{ijk})
to
(T,u_{i},u_{j},u_{k},u_{ij},u_{jk},u_{ki},\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!)
.
Since the tetrahedron polynomial
T
is multi-affine, it is possible to rewrite
u
in terms of
T
(and
u_{ijk}
in terms of
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!
) in the following way for the entire ABS list:

\displaystyle u=W(T,u_{ij},u_{jk},u_{ki},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k})\,,

\displaystyle u_{ijk}=W\big(\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!,u_{k},u_{i},u_{j},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k}\big)\,.

Here,
W
is a fraction with a numerator which is multi-affine in
(T,u_{ij},u_{jk},u_{ki})
and a denominator which is multi-affine in
(u_{ij},u_{jk},u_{ki})
.

Now we consider the action on an elementary cube of the cross 2-form, apply the variable transformation, and Taylor expand about
T=0
and
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!=0
:

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\displaystyle=-\Lambda(u_{i},u_{j},\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})+\Lambda\big(W(T,u_{ij},u_{jk},u_{ki},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}\big),u_{ij},\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=-\Lambda(u_{i},u_{j},\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{n!}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{T}^{n}}\Lambda\big(W(T,u_{ij},u_{jk},u_{ki},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}),u_{ij},\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}\big)\Big|_{T=0}\right)\,T^{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 341.43306pt-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,.

Now, the fact that
T,\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!=0\implies S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

means that the zeroth and first order terms must cancel against the terms involving

and

, so we have:

.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Xi}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{Q_{ij}}^{n}}\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}

{}{}

{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(V,u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})\Big|_{Q_{ij}=0}}{\displaystyle n!\left(\frac{\partial{T}}{\partial{u}}\frac{\mu(u_{i},u_{j},u_{jk},u_{ki},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k})}{\gamma(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k})}\right)^{n}}\,\left(\frac{\partial{\Omega_{1}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}\Omega_{2}-\frac{\partial{\Omega_{2}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}\Omega_{1}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 298.75394pt-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,,

where
V=V(Q_{ij},u_{i},u_{j},u_{ij},\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})
.

For the rest of the ABS list we can rewrite the expansion (

) using Equation (

) and can conclude that:

Proposition 19
.

For Equations Q1, Q2, Q3, H1, H2, H3 and A1, a double zero expansion for the cube action of the triangle 2-form in terms of
\Omega_{1}
,

is given by

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{Q_{ij}}^{n}}\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

.

These propositions give a possible variational interpretation to the octahedron equations. The double zero expansions imply that the equations
\Omega_{1}=0
and
\Omega_{2}=0
are sufficient conditions for criticality, hence they imply the corner equations.

5.5
Example: double zero expansions for H1

In this subsection we show that the general construction described above leads to succinct double zero expansions for the actions over the cube of each of the discrete 2-forms associated with H1. For this example, we will verify by explicit computation that the zeroth and first order terms vanish in the Taylor expansions.

Cross-square Lagrangian.

Solving the quad equation
Q_{ij}=0
for
u
, with
Q_{ij}
given by Equation (
15
), we find a change of variables expressing
u
in terms of the quad polynomial
Q_{ij}
:

u=\frac{\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}+Q_{ij}}{u_{i}-u_{j}}+u_{ij}\,.

Now we apply this to a single cross-square Lagrangian and Taylor expand in
Q_{ij}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle=uu_{i}+u_{j}u_{ij}-uu_{j}-u_{i}u_{ij}-(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log(u-u_{ij})-(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log(u_{i}-u_{j})\,,

\displaystyle=(u-u_{ij})(u_{i}-u_{j})-(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log\left((u-u_{ij})(u_{i}-u_{j})\right)+2\pi i\Xi(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\,,

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}+Q_{ij}-(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}+Q_{ij})+2\pi i\Xi(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\,

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})(1-\log(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}))+2\pi i\Xi(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})-\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{Q_{ij}^{n}}{n(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})^{n-1}}\,.

We find that the first order term vanishes as expected, but the zeroth order term does not. It depends only on the lattice parameters. When we consider the action around the cube, the zeroth order contributions cancel, except for terms of the form
2\pi i\Xi_{i}\alpha_{i}
:

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle=\!\overleftrightarrow{\,\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\,}\!-\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{Q_{ij}^{n}}{n(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})^{n-1}}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}-2\pi i(\Xi_{i}\alpha_{i}+\Xi_{j}\alpha_{j}+\Xi_{k}\alpha_{k})\,.

Hence,

S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}=\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{Q_{ij}^{n}}{n(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})^{n-1}}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,.

(56)

Equation (
56
) is a double zero expansion for the action around the cube of the cross square 2-form associated with H1 in terms of
Q_{ij}
,
Q_{jk}
,
Q_{ki}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,Q\,}\!_{ij}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,Q\,}\!_{jk}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,Q\,}\!_{ki}
.

Cross Lagrangian.

In order to derive a double zero expansion for the cross 2-form associated with H1, we consider a variable transformation for
u
in terms of
T
, obtained from Equation (
16
),

u=\frac{T-\big((\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})u_{jk}u_{ki}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\big)}{\big((\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})u_{ij}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\big)}\,.

From this we obtain

u-u_{ij}=\frac{T+(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})(u_{ij}-u_{jk})(u_{ki}-u_{ij})}{\big((\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})u_{ij}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\big)}\,.

(57)

We have an analogous transformation for
u_{ijk}
in terms of
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!
. Now we apply this to the action around the cube of the cross 2-form and Taylor expand in
T
and
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!
.
Using the point inversion symmetry we find

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=-(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log(u_{i}-u_{j})+(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log(u-u_{ij})-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log(u_{jk}-u_{ki})+(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log(u-u_{ij})-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log\!\left((u-u_{ij})(u_{jk}-u_{ki})\right)-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+2\pi i\Xi_{ij}(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,.

Now we substitute
u
using Equation (
57
):

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}

{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log\!\left(\frac{T(u_{jk}-u_{ki})+(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})(u_{ij}-u_{jk})(u_{ki}-u_{ij})(u_{jk}-u_{ki})}{\big((\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})u_{ij}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\big)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 241.84842pt-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+2\pi i\Xi_{ij}(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,.

Then we observe that

\displaystyle(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log\!\big((u_{ij}-u_{jk})(u_{ki}-u_{ij})(u_{jk}-u_{ki})\big)+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log\!\big((\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})u_{ij}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\big)+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=0\,,

because the logarithms are invariant under cyclic permutations of
i,j,k
, so we find

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\log\!\left(\frac{T}{(u_{ij}-u_{jk})(u_{ki}-u_{ij})}+(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})\right)-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle\hskip 213.39566pt-2\pi i(\Xi_{i}\alpha_{i}+\Xi_{j}\alpha_{j}+\Xi_{k}\alpha_{k})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{T^{n}}{n(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})^{n-1}(u_{ij}-u_{jk})^{n}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})^{n}}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle\hskip 213.39566pt-2\pi i(\Xi_{i}\alpha_{i}+\Xi_{j}\alpha_{j}+\Xi_{k}\alpha_{k})\,.

Hence

S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}=-\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{T^{n}}{n(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})^{n-1}(u_{ij}-u_{jk})^{n}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})^{n}}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

(58)

Note that the zeroth and first order terms vanish. Hence, Equation (
58
) is a double zero expansion in terms of
T
and
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!
.

Triangle Lagrangian.

In order to derive a double zero expansion for the action on the elementary cube of the triangle 2-form, we note the following relation for H1:

T=0\qquad\implies\qquad Q_{ij}=\frac{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})E_{ij}}{\left(\alpha_{i}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\right)}\,.

T,\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!=0\implies S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=0
, so we deduce form Equation (

) that

T,\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!=0\qquad\implies\qquad S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}

{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}=\frac{1}{2}S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\,.

Since
S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(u_{i},u_{j},u_{k},u_{ij},u_{ji},u_{ki})
does not depend on
u
or
u_{ijk}
, we can assume that
T=0
and
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,T\,}\!=0
, without affecting the action.
Thus, we can write the cube action for the triangle 2-form as

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})^{n-1}}\left(\frac{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})E_{ij}}{\left(\alpha_{i}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\right)}\right)^{n}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})(-1)^{n}}{n\left(\alpha_{i}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\right)^{n}}E_{ij}^{n}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,.

(59)

Equation (
59
) is a double zero expansion for the triangle 2-form in terms of
E_{i}
,
E_{j}
,
E_{k}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,E\,}\!_{i}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,E\,}\!_{j}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,E\,}\!_{k}
.

We can use the identities (
45
), where
g_{1}=1
for H1, to rewrite this in terms of
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
:

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})(-1)^{n}}{n\left(\alpha_{i}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\right)^{n}}\left(\frac{\frac{\partial{\Omega_{1}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}\Omega_{2}-\frac{\partial{\Omega_{2}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}\Omega_{1}}{\frac{\partial{\Omega_{1}}}{\partial{u_{ij}}}-\frac{\partial{\Omega_{1}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}\right)^{n}-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,.

(60)

Equation (
60
) is a double zero expansion for the triangle 2-form in terms of the octahedron polynomials.

Trident Lagrangian.

To derive the double zero expansion for action of the trident 2-form, we consider equation (
55
), which shows that cyclic combinations of quad equations lead to the tetrahedron equation. For H1, this can be written explicitly as

T=-(u-u_{jk})(u-u_{ki})Q_{ij}-(u-u_{ki})(u-u_{ij})Q_{jk}-(u-u_{ij})(u-u_{jk})Q_{ki}.

Using Equation (
54
), we can write the action on an elementary cube of the trident 2-form as

\displaystyle S_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 5.91 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\left(\frac{Q_{ij}^{n}}{n(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})^{n-1}}-\frac{T^{n}}{n(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})^{n-1}(u_{ij}-u_{jk})^{n}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})^{n}}\right)-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\left(\frac{Q_{ij}^{n}}{n(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})^{n-1}}-\frac{(-Q_{ij}(u-u_{jk})(u-u_{ki})+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk})^{n}}{n(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j})^{n-1}(u_{ij}-u_{jk})^{n}(u_{ij}-u_{ki})^{n}}\right)-{\,\overleftrightarrow{\!(\cdots)\!}\,}+{\circlearrowleft}_{ijk}\,.

(61)

Equation (
61
) is a double zero expansion for the cube action of the trident 2-form in terms of the quad polynomials
Q_{ij}
,
Q_{jk}
,
Q_{ki}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,Q\,}\!_{ij}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,Q\,}\!_{jk}
,
\!\overleftrightarrow{\,Q\,}\!_{ki}
.

Conclusion

In this work we compared the Lagrangian multiforms

,
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

and
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

on four-point stencils to the three-point one
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

that has been favoured in the previous literature on Lagrangian multiforms for the ABS equations.

In Part I
[
17
]
we focused on
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 5.91 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
: we introduced integer fields into the action to deal with the branch cuts of the logarithm and dilogarithm functions that occur in
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 5.91 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
, and showed that these integer fields are essential to obtain the closure property of Lagrangian multiforms.

In Part II, we showed that the same construction applies to
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 5.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 5.91 0 L 0 5.91 L 0 0 L 5.91 0 L 5.91 5.91 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\hbox to4.67pt{\vbox to4.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 L 0 0 L 0 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
. We established that the closure property holds for each of them, in the sense that the action over the cube vanishes (modulo
4\pi^{2}
) on solutions of the corresponding corner equations.
We studied the relations between the respective systems of corner equations using the relations between the Lagrangian 2-forms. We showed that the quad equations are equivalent to the combined system of tetrahedron and octahedron equations. In addition, we formulated the double-zero property, which has recently seen a lot of emphasis in the continuous and semi-discrete settings, on the discrete level. This gives a variational interpretation of the octahedron equations.

This work shows once again that Lagrangian multiform theory is a powerful variational principle. As well as being an attribute of integrability, it can provide a solution to the inverse problem of the calculus of variations for equations that do not admit a Lagrangian in the traditional sense.

The Lagrangian multiforms presented here are specific to the ABS equations, relying in particular on their three-leg forms. Lagrangian multiforms exists for other integrable quad equations, such as the discrete Gel’fand-Dikii equations
[
9
]
, but these do no have a three-leg structure. It remains to be seen if such examples also admit different Lagrangian multiforms producing distinct but related corner equations.

The double-zero property has been observed in Lagrangian multiforms for non-ABS lattice equations
[
11
,
15
]
. Since the corner equations imply that the gradient of the action vanishes, we expect the double-zero property to hold for all discrete Lagrangian multiforms that satisfy the closure property. A careful study of the closure property for non-ABS examples, generalising the results of Part I, is left for future work.
